One of the best upgrades you can make to your PC gaming setup is an OLED monitor. Fortunately, MSI’s factory-reconditioned Mag 272QP X24 is on sale at Woot through November 18th with a 180-day manufacturer warranty for $290.99, which is more than $100 cheaper than the full MSRP of $399.99.

MSI’s 27-inch screen is well-featured for the price, with 1440p resolution, a 240Hz refresh rate, and both G-Sync and FreeSync compatibility, which help reduce screen tearing when your FPS is below the max refresh rate. PC games are particularly well-suited to take advantage of the deep black levels OLED panels produce, too, giving every game you play a deeper, more immersive feel. MSI’s budget OLED also features quantum dots for fuller, more vivid colors. It doesn’t cut corners on ports either, with two HDMI 2.1, one DisplayPort 1.4a, and one USB Type-C DP Alt input.

Forget ‘Ultrasound Money’? Vitalik Buterin Pushes New Ethereum Store-of-Value Vision

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Ethereum co-creator Vitalik Buterin is challenging one of Ether’s most celebrated investment narratives, suggesting that the second-largest crypto’s long-term value should depend less on how many tokens the network burns and more on its ability to become a reliable store of value.

The remarks could signal a major shift in how Ethereum’s value proposition is presented, particularly as declining transaction-fee burns weaken the deflationary narrative that once made ETH attractive to investors.

In a Sunday morning post on X, Buterin outlined three main reasons for shifting attention away from Ethereum’s fee-burning narrative and toward its potential as a store of value.

First, he argued that ETH’s deflationary appeal was never as powerful as it seemed. Although the 2021 EIP-1559 upgrade introduced a mechanism to burn a portion of transaction fees, the amount of ETH destroyed has not always been enough to offset new token issuance. In his view, making fee burns the central measure of Ethereum’s success places too much emphasis on a single aspect of its token economics. According to Buterin, even if fee burns remain below 10% of the level required to offset new issuance indefinitely, Buterin said, “we should not feel sad,” adding that “the value of ETH comes from elsewhere.”

Another concern is that prioritizing ETH as a fee-burning asset could push Ethereum toward decisions that undermine its core principles. Buterin suggested that designing the network primarily to maximize transaction-fee burns could create tensions with decentralization and censorship resistance. Such an approach could require trade-offs that make the network less open and less resistant to external control, potentially weakening the very qualities that distinguish Ethereum from competing blockchain networks.

By contrast, positioning ETH as a store of value would reinforce Ethereum’s existing strengths rather than force the network to pursue aggressive fee-burning targets. Buterin pointed to decentralization and what he described as CROPS (censorship resistance, open source, privacy, and security) as the qualities that give Ethereum its distinctive appeal. He argued these characteristics provide a more sustainable foundation for ETH’s long-term value than relying heavily on transaction fees to reduce its circulating supply.

Notably, the fee-burning mechanism was central to Ethereum’s popular “ultrasound money” narrative, which gained significant traction among ETH investors during the 2021 bull run. The thesis suggested that by permanently removing a portion of transaction fees from circulation, Ethereum could become increasingly deflationary, potentially making ETH scarcer and more valuable over time.

At the heart of Buterin’s argument is the idea that Ethereum’s greatest contribution to the broader financial ecosystem is stability. Its decentralization, censorship resistance, privacy, and security are not simply ideological advantages but practical qualities that matter to individuals and institutions holding assets on the network. A blockchain that can provide predictable, reliable infrastructure without depending on centralized intermediaries could become increasingly attractive as financial markets evolve.

He also sees an opportunity in the rapid expansion of artificial intelligence. As AI continues to disrupt industries and reshape economic activity, dependable financial infrastructure could become more valuable. “In the era of AI disruption, stability will be highly valued,” he wrote.

Buterin believes Ethereum’s emphasis on stability could give ETH a stronger store-of-value proposition in this changing environment, shifting the investment narrative away from token burns and toward the network’s ability to preserve trust over the long term.

Interestingly, Buterin’s position also found support from Solana co-founder Anatoly Yakovenko, who appeared to share his skepticism about making transaction fees the centerpiece of a blockchain’s value proposition. Yakovenko dismissed the idea of treating fees as the product, calling it “absolutely meaningless.” He argued that blockchain networks should focus on delivering tangible value to users rather than trying to control how much people pay to have their transactions included in a block.

The 25 Sexiest Movies on Prime Video Right Now

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Including the best erotic thrillers, raunchy comedies, and all the mess in between.

Even in just the past decade, sexy cinema has come a long way from Fifty Shades of Grey. No judgement here—the entire trilogy is available to stream on Prime Video if you haven’t seen the Dakota Johnson and Jamie Dornan-starring franchise. But if you’re looking to expand your range a bit, we have a lot to recommend.

Sexy movies are having a comeback in 2026, thanks to Wuthering Heights, I Want Your Sex, and—another Olivia Wilde film—The Invite. While Emerald Fennell adapted Emily Bronte’s novel into a tortured and depraved sexual experience, Wilde’s I Want Your Sex and The Invite used sex more as a vehicle for dark comedy. But it’s not just new films on this list. We can also point you to Weekend, Ghost, Before Sunrise, and just any kind of sexy movie you’re looking for on screen.

Below, you’ll find our pick of the 25 sexiest movies you can stream on Prime Video right now.

Olivia Wilde and Cooper Hoffman star in Gregg Araki’s raunchy workplace romantic comedy about a high profile artist (Wilde) who ropes one of her new assistants (Hoffman) into a dominant/submissive relationship. At first, he enjoys the attention, but the rollercoaster of their sexual power dynamic eventually drives them both insane.

Sydney Sweeney does Rear Window meets Peeping Tom in this sexy crime drama about a couple who becomes obsessed with the sex lives of the people they can see from their apartment window across the street. The film also stars Justice Smith and Ahsoka’s Natasha Liu Bordizzo.

No Hard Feelings follows a down-on-her-luck Uber driver (Jennifer Lawrence) who is hired by a very wealthy couple to take their inexperienced nineteen-year-old son (Andrew Barth Feldman) on a date to boost his confidence before he leaves for college. The film treads dangerous waters, but Lawrence won over the audience and earned herself a Golden Globe nomination for the sexy comedy.

Love her or hate her, Emerald Fennell can make a steamy movie. Wuthering Heights is a little more of a reimagining than an adaptation, taking quite a few liberties with the text, but it puts the romantic and sexual dynamics right at the forefront of the story. And there’s no beating Margot Robbie and Jacob Elordi as the leads.

The Fault in Our Stars director Josh Boone teams up with It Ends With Us novelist Colleen Hoover for a romance drama about a teen pregnancy that alters the course of two lovers’ lives. That is, until later in life, when the couple (Allison Wiliams and Dave Franco) reunite to explore what could have been.

Emerald Fennell’s steamy The Talented Mr. Ripley–esque thriller, Saltburn, follows a young student at Oxford University who becomes infatuated (read: dangerously obsessed) with one of his aristocratic classmates, who invites him to his family’s grand estate. It’s a darkly funny and wildly intense ride.

The phenom that saved dark romance novels: Fifty Shades of Grey and the entire Fifty Shades trilogy is available to stream on Prime Video. The story, for those who still don’t know, follows a recent college graduate (Dakota Johnson) who signs a contract to take part in a sexual bondage relationship with billionaire Christian Gray (Jamie Dornan).

L’inconnu du lac (known as Stranger by the Lake) is one of the most acclaimed erotic thrillers of the 21st century, picking up Best Director and the Queer Palm awards in the year it premiered at the Cannes Film Festival. In Stranger by the Lake, Franck, a man in his 30s, meets Michel by a lake where gay men often bathe and immediately falls for him. What he doesn’t know, however, is that Michel is a psychopathic murderer. Stranger by the Lake is as smart as it is sexy, and it will truly keep you on your toes.

Who said rom-coms can’t be sexy too? Secretary charts the dominant-submissive relationship that blossoms between a lawyer and his secretary. It’s certainly on the crazier side and may not be for everyone, but Secretary is a brilliantly acted film that feels quite far ahead of its time for such a mainstream movie in its original take on a romantic BDSM relationship.

Carol does something a little different: It’s a slow-burning romance with sensuality bubbling beneath the surface. It follows a department-store worker and aspiring photographer whose life takes an unexpected turn when she falls in love with a mysterious, beautiful older woman going through a difficult divorce. Todd Haynes’s Oscar-nominated period romance is a masterpiece of modern queer cinema, with career-defining performances from Rooney Mara and Cate Blanchett. Hugely romantic and remarkably executed, Carol is a special film that’ll certainly stick with you.

Alongside Four Weddings and a Funeral, Sirens was one of the films that brought generational British heartthrob Hugh Grant to American audiences. Grant plays a progressive reverend who, with his wife, pays a visit to an eccentric artist whose work is viewed as blasphemous. His wife soon finds herself drawn to the three models working with the painter, and what ensues is a clever breakdown of 1930s sexual repression.

Everyone loves a meet-cute, and Weekend’s is as brilliant as they come. Russell thinks he’s signed up for a one-night stand with Glen, a man he meets in a club, but over the course of a single weekend, a deeper relationship quickly grows between the two. Touching, oddly realistic, and distinctly British in its sense of humor, Weekend is sexy, funny, and grounded in a way that few romantic comedy-dramas manage.

In Eternity, Elizabeth Olsen plays a woman who, upon reaching the afterlife, is forced to choose between two people to spend eternity with: her first husband, who died young, and her second husband of 65 years. Eternity is infectiously sweet, a little sexy, and an essential modern comedy.

It might be a stretch to call this one “sexy,” per se, but you won’t find a romantic drama quite like this one—and you definitely won’t find one that’s more intense. Luca Guadagnino’s Bones and All is a road-trip drama that follows a young woman who develops feelings for a disillusioned young drifter on a 3,000-mile journey across America. Oh, and they’re both cannibals.

Wong Kar-wai’s romantic masterpiece proves that a film can be truly romantic, sensual, and intense while still holding solidly on to a PG rating. In the Mood for Love follows a man and a woman who discover that their spouses are having an affair with one another, but as they bond, they begin to develop their own connection. This is one for the yearners.

Sexy films are by no means a new phenomenon, and Caligula is one of the originals. Though the original was pretty poorly received, the film recently received a restoration and rerelease that reconstructed the film entirely from footage that didn’t make the final cut.

Love triangles don’t get much more complicated than this, and sports dramas don’t get much sexier. Challengers follows two friends who fall in love with the same young tennis prodigy and the complicated chaos that ensues when their relationships get messy. Tennis has never looked so thrilling

Set in the dreamy Italian countryside, Call Me by Your Name remains the film that introduced Timothée Chalamet to the masses as a future leading man (and got him his first Oscar nomination). Here he plays a teenager who falls in love with his father’s assistant and comes to terms with his sexuality. The film is sad, symbolic, and deeply romantic.

The legendary leading duo of Patrick Swayze and Demi Moore speaks for itself. Ghost is maybe a little more on the sentimental side than some of the other “sexy films” in Prime Video’s lineup, but it’s a deeply moving one worth your time.

Portrait of a Lady on Fire was an immediate hit with critics and audiences when it was released in 2019. The film follows an artist commissioned to paint an aristocratic heiress, but things become more complicated when the two fall in love. Romantic films don’t get more French than this one, which is also loosely based on the timeless tale of Orpheus and Eurydice.

Nicole Kidman and Harris Dickinson are an absolutely electrifying onscreen pair in this erotic thriller that sees a CEO begin an affair with her power-hungry intern, putting the stability of her family life and career on the line. Kidman picked up the Best Actress award at the Venice Film Festival for her role, and it’s been called one of her most ambitious performances to date.

Can anything capture your heart like a meet-cute romance? American traveler Jesse and French student Céline unceremoniously meet on a train from Budapest to Vienna, deciding on a whim to spend his last hours on the continent together. It’s the first in Richard Linklater’s acclaimed Before trilogy, and it sets you up for a brilliant slow burn.

If a Skarsgård signed up for it, I’m interested. He’s about to play a love interest made out of wicker, but last year Alexander Skarsgård played the mysteriously sexy biker-gang leader Ray in Pillion. The romantic black comedy tells the story of a shy man who begins a dominant-submissive relationship with the aforementioned biker, and it traces his personal growth as he navigates their dynamic. Pillion is sexy, crazy, and downright hilarious. It’s British black comedy at its finest.

Starring the king of crazy, surprisingly sexy indie movies, James Spader himself, Sex, Lies and Videotape is a true cult classic. The film revolves around a married couple with intimacy problems whose lives are changed when a mysterious drifter with a peculiar interest comes to stay with them.

David Cronenberg’s notorious erotic thriller is perhaps the most transgressive entry on this list, following a group of people who become sexually fixated on car crashes. Adapted from J.G. Ballard’s novel and starring James Spader, Holly Hunter, and Deborah Kara Unger, Crash is disturbing, provocative, and about as daring as erotic cinema gets.

Julia Child’s Purple-Hued Cambridge Home Sells Just Days After Listing

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

The legendary TV chef’s Massachusetts house fetched $6.525 million—above the asking price.

In today’s real estate market, price cuts are increasingly common. But Julia Child’s former Cambridge home just bucked the trend, selling mere days after listing—and fetching above the asking price. The legendary TV chef’s longtime Massachusetts residence hit the market on September 23 for $6.475 million. By October 2, it had found a buyer. The sale officially closed on October 29, per Zillow. In the end, the six-bedroom Cape Cod–style house traded hands for $6.525 million, $50,000 above asking.

Child and her husband, Paul, lived in the house for decades, starting in 1961, after Paul retired from his job in the federal government. The estate is also where Child shot several popular TV shows, among them In Julia’s Kitchen with Master Chefs and Julia and Jacques Cooking at Home.

Painted a muted shade of purple, the 6,300-square-foot home sits on a quarter-acre lot just a few blocks from Harvard Yard. Dating to 1889, the residence at one time featured a custom kitchen designed especially for Child, with pegboard walls, a six-burner industrial Garland range, and maple countertops a few inches taller than average to suit her 6’3” frame.

In the wake of Paul’s 1994 death, Child continued to live in the residence until 2001, when she donated the property to Smith College, her alma mater. (The Massachusetts institution sold the home in 2002 for $2.35 million, per Zillow.) The famous kitchen, meanwhile, was meticulously deconstructed—down to its linoleum flooring—and rebuilt in D.C.’s National Museum of American History, where it remains on display today.

Since then, the new owners have revamped the three-story home’s kitchen, which now sports marble countertops, a metallic backsplash, Poggenpohl cabinetry, and a Sub-Zero refrigerator. The rest of the residence is equipped with light-filled interiors, hardwood floors, and eco-friendly additions like solar panels.

This summer, the Cambridge City Council passed a proposal to install a historical marker on the estate’s grounds, according to Boston.com.

By chance, another Child-linked property recently traded hands as well, albeit for a discount. In August, a Santa Barbara ranch where she filmed the 12-episode PBS series Dinner at Julia’s listed for just under $22 million. It sold on October 16 for $19 million.

Geoffrey Montes is an associate editor at ELLE Decor with a serious love for all things real estate and design. Before that, he worked at Architectural Digest, Galerie, and Preservation magazines, covering everything from jaw-dropping listings to world-famous architects and design events like Salone del Mobile and Homo Faber.

6 Pieces of Wearable Tech That Solve Real-World Problems

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

Health tracking, AI-powered notes, and more—behold, the most useful accessories on the market.

At this point, it’s normal to have a wearable, something like an Apple Watch or Fitbit, or an Oura ring to track your sleep and activity levels. You’ve maybe also heard of Whoop, pioneer of the screenless tracker. At the end of the day, all these devices do the same thing to varying degrees of success. If your focus is on training then the Whoop band is made for you, if you’re outdoorsy you have Garmin, and if you want to get better sleep maybe try Oura. But if you have other, more specific goals, it might seem like there’s not a lot out there for you. But worry not, all that is changing, and it’s changing fast.

These devices represent the latest breakthroughs in wearable technology, going beyond the basics and swinging for the fences. From rings and bands dedicated to blood pressure monitoring to an AI note-taker that you clip on your lapel, these aren’t trying to become your new all-in-one wearables. Instead, they are each trying to be a tool for those who need it most. With an eye toward mindfulness and focus, instead of overwhelming with data, these unique wearables go beyond just tracking sleep and activity level. These devices will make you think maybe the future really is here, and not so bad after all.

If you have one of many medical conditions (I genuinely do) that requires you to monitor your blood pressure you know how annoying those cuffs can get. Hilo suggests a better solution; Hilo Core is dedicated to providing users with insights into their blood pressure over time. As a result, the system is a bit more complicated than just a single wearable with an app. That’s the main part, but the FDA-cleared blood pressure cuff is the third part of the equation. The three aspects of the Hilo work in tandem to give accurate, consistent blood pressure readings with as little invasiveness as possible.

To start, you need to use the cuff to take baseline readings for a few days but after that you should only have to use the cuff once per month to calibrate your readings. After that, you can rely on the Hilo Band itself for accurate and consistent readings thanks to its advanced optical sensors. All of that is viewable in graphs and charts that put your hourly, daily, and weekly readings into a greater context.

Hilo isn’t the only company making wearables that are trying to replace chunky old-fashioned blood pressure cuffs. And unlike Hilo, Signal even does it without needing any kind of cuff at any point. All you need is the single Signal ring, and no, there’s no subscription to pay.

Compared to an Oura ring and other similar watches and bands, the Signal Ring uses more advanced sensors to capture your systolic and diastolic readings to display your blood pressure in real time. It’s not measuring general cardiovascular strain or anything vague like that, it’s actually measuring blood pressure. And like with the Hilo, same situation; you can view everything on the app.

It’s worth noting the device itself is not yet FDA cleared (and neither is the Hilo Band to be clear). A 451-person study the company commissioned did meet the requirements of ISO 81060-2, the international accuracy standard for non-invasive blood pressure devices.

This isn’t a watch or ring, but it definitely works best as a wearable. Plaud makes AI powered audio recorders that can also automatically transcribe everything for you. As a writer and editor, this is somewhere AI could have saved me time in my intern days.

The NotePin S is the size of a lapel microphone and clips right on to your shirt just like one too. At least that’s my favorite way to wear it—feels professional. You can wear it like a necklace or in a wrist band too, all options that are possible with attachments that come in the box.

I’ve written about vagus nerve stimulators before. It’s an underwritten trend, and the mindfulness tech that’s been coming out over the past year or two has been embracing it. Essentially, the vagus nerve runs from the brainstem all the way down to, really, every other major organ in your body. vagus nerve stimulation devices send electrical signals that can supposedly fix many ailments. In my experience, they are great for relaxation and can help with anxiety and stress relief. But really, it’s meant to aid your parasympathetic nervous system. This impacts your digestion, and is also the source of your “fight or flight” response.

Like other handheld devices I’ve used, you have to apply a layer of electricity conducting gel before a session with the Pulsetto. After that, put it on (it’s adjustable to any neck size) and let the electrodes work their magic. It’s certainly a well-built, futuristic feeling piece of tech, but as with any of these actual results may vary.

Pulse takes a different approach to smart rings. It’s not about becoming the best version of yourself by optimizing your sleep, fitness, and recovery every second of the day. Instead, this is a ring that senses your stress levels throughout the day and gives you vibrations (pulses) when you might, based on your vitals, need a breather. Be it meditation or just a minute away to get your composure, sometimes taking this time can be the difference between turning a bad day around and having it go worse.

Like the Pulsetto device, Sona is making a vagus nerve stimulator. This one goes on your ear like a single earbud. From there it sends stimulating signals to your vagus nerve, no goop required. Once you’ve onboarded the device and set up your protocol, the promise is that the AI is always adapting to you and your body’s rhythms.

Sona’s focuses are stress relief and better sleep. Yes, you are supposed to sleep with it on. Full disclosure, I can’t say I’ve tried this one and for nearly $1,000 I don’t want to recommend it wholeheartedly without hands-on time. But the promise is... well promising, compared to other similar devices out there, even if the price makes it currently unattainable for most.

This is far from all that’s out there. I’ve seen smart rings like Kiwear that control your phone with a flick of your finger. Or similar rings that let you control the display on your smart glasses. I’ve tried exoskeletons, like the upcoming Hypershell Halo, that genuinely feel like a robot is moving your body for you. And that’s just scratching the surface—we have a long way to go, and plenty of concerns to address along the way, but wearable tech isn’t going anywhere.

Cardano goes on-chain with criminal evidence tracking for police in India’s most populous state

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Cardano (ADA) is expanding its real-world utility after police in Uttar Pradesh, India’s most populous state, began using the blockchain to track forensic evidence in active criminal cases.

The Cardano Foundation announced the deployment on Monday, revealing that every evidence scan and laboratory result is stored on-chain to strengthen record integrity during investigations.

Notably, TrivolveTech developed the Forensic Management System (FMS) in collaboration with Quixy. The project uses Cardano as an underlying verification layer, allowing police personnel to continue working through a conventional application without directly interacting with blockchain infrastructure.

The system addresses a potential weakness in conventional forensic procedures, where a shared case number can expose evidence to interference as it moves between police officers and separate laboratories. TrivolveTech designed FMS to replace that common identifier with unique codes for individual evidence handlers, limiting the ability to link records across different stages of an investigation.

Moreover, evidence boxes carry secure QR codes that can only be read through the FMS application. Each scan creates a record associated with the responsible handler, while cryptographic hashes are anchored to Cardano. These hashes function as digital fingerprints, allowing investigators to identify changes to recorded information during subsequent verification.

“Police evidence handling depends on trust between departments that are deliberately kept separate,” TrivolveTech founder and CEO Rahul Konudula said. “Each handler now works from an identifier meaningful only to their own step, and every scan becomes a permanent record on Cardano,” he added.

However, the system cannot confirm that evidence was collected properly or prevent physical tampering. Its value lies in the attributable digital trail it creates, which can show how evidence was handled and whether its records have been changed.

The Uttar Pradesh deployment adds to Cardano’s growing list of applications beyond cryptocurrency transactions. Last Wednesday, the Cardano Foundation reported that more than 100,000 farms in India had been registered on the blockchain through an agricultural initiative associated with the Syngenta Foundation. The project aims to make agricultural records more verifiable and reusable across lending, insurance, and government services.

Cardano is also pursuing education initiatives. The next day, the foundation announced that Cardano Academy would contribute a session on Cardano fundamentals and its extended unspent transaction output model to the University of Nicosia’s online Blockchain and Digital Assets course.

That said, Cardano is not the only blockchain network being used to secure official records. In Kenya, the Kenya National Examinations Council (KNEC) recently launched a blockchain-based credential verification system using Avalanche, with more than 30 million academic records reported to have been moved onto the network.

The initiative aims to help employers, universities, and other institutions verify academic credentials while making fraudulent certificates easier to identify.

Skydance outlines its $6 billion in planned cost savings. Here's the breakdown

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Skydance is pulling back the curtain on how it plans to achieve at least $6 billion in cost savings in the next three years.

"By combining two global streaming services, two film and TV studio groups, two television portfolios, and overlapping corporate functions, we can reduce duplicative spend across an expense base of more than $50 billion," the company wrote in a letter to shareholders Monday as part of Paramount Skydance's third-quarter earnings release.

Skydance, the company formed by the combination of Paramount Skydance and Warner Bros. Discovery, said it projects at least $2.2 billion in cost reductions from eliminating "redundant back-office and corporate functions," including eliminating management layers and ramping the use of artificial intelligence. Those savings also account for "rationalizing" its office footprint.

Skydance expects at least $1 billion in savings from streamlining marketing and promotional spending. And around $1.4 billion in savings will come from unifying its streaming businesses and broadcast architecture and integrating back-end technology.

A final $1.4 billion in savings will be tied to "optimizing third-party spend," eliminating overlapping expenses, and consolidating purchasing decisions to one centralized group, Skydance said.

"We are confident in achieving these savings, drawing on our playbook from Paramount," the company wrote. "That program is on track to achieve over $2.7 billion in run-rate efficiencies by the end of 2026."

Some of that cost-cutting has already begun.

Last month, Skydance began offering voluntary buyouts to employees ahead of official layoffs. At the same time, the company adopted a five-day-per-week in-office policy. Additionally, last week saw the departure of eight executives — four from Warner Bros. and four from Paramount.

The company has previously said it plans to eventually merge the Paramount+ and HBO Max streaming services into a single platform.

On Monday, Skydance said it would take the first step toward that goal early next year, offering a bundle of the streaming services "at an even greater value."

Co-CEO Ynon Kreiz told investors during the company's earnings call that Skydance is also looking for ways to optimize its content budget. The company spends more than $30 billion annually on shows and films.

"As one company, we can make smarter choices about how we distribute and monetize both new content and library content," Kreiz said.

Skydance noted that it's expecting a $200 million headwind in the fourth quarter from theatrical underperformances at Warner Bros. studio related to already launched titles and upcoming releases.

The company will also utilize AI to speed up production and decrease costs to make films, co-CEO and chairman David Ellison said on the call.

"We think these tools will help unlock more original storytelling by being able to make the budget more digestible to be able to take that amount of risk," Ellison said.

The updates came alongside third-quarter results for the legacy Paramount Skydance company that included revenue of $7.03 billion, which was roughly in line with StreetAccount estimates of $7.04 billion, and adjusted earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation and amortization of $1 billion, topping StreetAccount estimates of $913.5 million.

However, adjusted earnings per share of 45 cents were not immediately comparable to StreetAccount EPS estimates.

The results don't include the impact of the Warner Bros. Discovery acquisition, which closed after the end of the third quarter.

Skydance detailed business units of the combined entity, including studios, direct-to-consumer and TV, and said it will provide full-year guidance during its fourth-quarter earnings report early next year.

"Going forward, we will provide guidance on an annual basis, as we prioritize long-term value creation, which we believe will be more consistent with our strategic approach," the company told shareholders. "We will continue to provide quarterly updates, including certain forward-looking commentary where applicable, which will give investors visibility into our near-term progress of the business."

Mars' Molten Heart is Long Gone, but Some Magma Chambers May Persist

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

We'll never stop being fascinated by Mars. It's kind of like a failed Earth. It appears as if billions of years ago Mars was warm, wet, and habitable. That ended when its interior cooled and its geodynamo failed.

When its geodynamo failed, its magnetic shield failed. The full force of the Sun bore down on the unfortunate planet, and its warming atmosphere was lost to space.

The loss of atmosphere wasn't the only change. Mars had a powerful volcanic past, and the prominent volcanoes in Tharsis Montes prove that. But the planet is largely believed to have left its volcanic past behind as a consequence of cooling.

But there's growing evidence that the planet isn't totally dormant. Recent evidence from missions like InSight and the ExoMars Trace Gas Orbiter suggest some ongoing activity. InSight detected more than 1300 Marsquakes, and the Trace Gas Orbiter detected hydrochloric acid.

Hydrochloric acid (HCl) is a highly corrosive acid with multiple natural sources, including atmospheric ones. The ExoMars Trace Gas Orbiter (TGO) collected widespread observations of HCl in Mars' atmosphere at parts-per-billion levels, but initially, it was only detected during perihelion. The HCl detections were attributed to aerosol-atmospheric sources driven by greater warmth from the Sun, a naturally-occurring, seasonal pattern.

But new research in Science Advances shows that not all of the TGO's HCl detections can be traced to perihelion. It's titled "Enigmatic HCl detections as a potential signature of igneous activity on Mars." The lead author is Alexander Trokhimovskiy from the Space Research Institute at the Russian Academy of Sciences.

This Mars Orbiter Laser Altimeter (MOLA) image shows Alba Mons near the top, with the three Tharsis Montes volcanoes below it, and the massive Olympus Mons to their northwest. Though not as tall as Olympus Mons, Alba Mons is larger by area. The new HCl detections are curiously close to Alba Mons. Image Credit: NASA/JPL/GSFC

The authors acknowledge that Mars is believed to be volcanically dormant, and that Marsquakes and other evidence of more recent volcanic activity is accumulating. However, there's no clear understanding of Mars' modern activity. For instance, many of the InSight lander's detections were attributed to meteorites.

If we were to witness an actual eruption, and the gases that go with it, a new understanding of Mar's interior would arise. But that hasn't happened, and we have to rely on less obvious evidence for Mars' internal state.

"While sulfur dioxide, so far undetected, could testify to ongoing eruptive activity, evidence of active magma chambers may be better identified by seepage of halogen gases, such as hydrogen chloride (HCl)," the authors write. "Here, we report HCl detections during the cold aphelion season that are isolated in the Alba Mons region." Could these new HCl detections be a window into Mars' interior?

Olympus Mons is known as the largest volcano and the tallest mountain in the Solar System. But Alba Mons is actually the largest volcano by surface area. Like Olympus Mons, it's also in the Tharsis region. (And neither are part of Tharsis Montes, the iconic row of three volcanoes). Alba Mons' volcanic flows reach further than 1300 km from its central summit. One of Alba Mons most significant features is its enormous system of fractures. For multiple reasons, detecting HCl over Alba Mons is intriguing.

"Strongly localized in space and time, aphelion HCl detections, amidst an otherwise absence of HCl at that season on Mars, exhibit marked contrast with prolonged and widespread detections at perihelion," the authors write.

âDetections of HCl at low altitudes, unexplained by observed atmospheric state and models, together with the geological context of the region, hint at an igneous origin of this gas,â Trokhimovskiy et al. write. These most recent HCl directions were in 2025.

This figure show HCl detections by the Trace Gas Orbiter. Blue ones represent perihelion detections, which can be explained by seasonal warming and atmospheric interactions driven by the Sun. Red ones are more enigmatic. They could come from tiny fissures near Alba Mons, almost directly under the detections. Image Credit: Alexander Trokhimovskiy et al. , Enigmatic HCl detections as a potential signature of igneous activity on Mars. Sci. Adv.12, eaeb9422(2026).

The HCl detections alone may not be enough to oveturn the idea that Mars is dormant. As the authors say, they're just a hint. But there's more. "We also identify fresh and narrow (<1 meter) fissures in this area, which we interpret to be potential HCl pathways rooted into magmatic bodies at depth," the researchers explain.

"These structures have not been identified on Mars in earlier works. For instance, systematic geomorphologic mapping of the northern hemisphere did not consider these landforms," the authors explain.

These panels shows some of the important points in the research. (A) shows Alba Mons and the HCl aphelion detections in yellow. (B) is a CASSIS image from the TGO showing a fractured ridge, while (C) and (D) are HiRISE images from NASA's Mars Reconnaissance Orbiter showing smaller fissures the researchers identify as potential HCl sources. (E) and (F) are geological models that can explain HCl is seeping from these fissures. Image Credit: Alexander Trokhimovskiy et al. , Enigmatic HCl detections as a potential signature of igneous activity on Mars. Sci. Adv.12, eaeb9422(2026).

The researchers calculated the seepage rate necessary to account for the HCl abundance in the aphelion detections. They compared it to the perihelion detections, and included the expected lifetime for HCl to persist in the Martian atmosphere. Their work rules out atmospheric-aerosol interactions as the source for the HCl detected during aphelion. But it also rules out those same interactions for the source of HCl detected during perihelion. That makes sense, since non-seasonal seepage from magma chambers would be happening no matter the season.

"Thus, perihelion HCl is unlikely to be directly volcanic in origin," they write. There could still be a link between igneous HCl and perihelion HCl. "However, a connection between igneous and seasonal HCl may still exist via a mechanism complementary to heterogeneous production," the authors write.

Planets are complicated, as we Earthlings know. Multiple interrelated factors contribute to the whole, and everything changes over time. This work further emphasizes how that's true, even on so-called dormant Mars. "The overall cycle of hydrogen chloride includes irreversible sinks," the authors write, and sinks have to be included in any modelling or understanding of Mars.

"Introducing a volcanic source of HCl, we can propose that a fraction of the perihelion HCl observed in the Martian atmosphere is ultimately of igneous origin," the researchers explain. "Localized igneous sources may therefore act as continuous primary contributors of chlorine, which is subsequently redistributed between atmospheric and surface reservoirs."

But despite the complicated picture of seasonality and surface sinks, their conclusion is clear: "Our results are best explained by ongoing igneous processes in the Martian interior."

Evan Gough is a science-loving guy with no formal education who loves Earth, forests, hiking, and heavy music. He's guided by Carl Sagan's quote: "Understanding is a kind of ecstasy."

South Korean Exchanges May Now Store 99% of Customer Assets in Cold Wallets Post $6.3B Binance Hack

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

South Korea’s financial regulator is weighing stricter custody rules for cryptocurrency exchanges after the record $6.3 billion Binance hack, according to reports of an emergency meeting held this week.

The Financial Services Commission convened major domestic platforms, the Financial Supervisory Service and industry groups on November 2nd to review security practices after unauthorized withdrawals from Binance hot wallets. They urged exchanges to raise the proportion of customer assets kept in cold storage, offline and disconnected from the internet, alongside tighter access controls, continuous monitoring of deposits and withdrawals, and stronger incident-response measures.

Under existing rules in the Virtual Asset User Protection Act, exchanges must already hold at least 80% of user assets in cold wallets, yet discussions at the meeting explored pushing that threshold significantly higher, with some reports indicating consideration of a level around 99%. Major platforms already operate at different ratios, as Upbit has reported more than 99% offline, while others range from the mid-80s to mid-90s.

No final rule change has been announced, and the FSC’s public statements emphasize the need for greater offline holdings to limit exposure in the event of a breach without specifying a new mandatory percentage.

Higher cold-wallet requirements could reduce the liquid assets available for immediate withdrawals, potentially affecting smaller exchanges more than larger ones that already maintain elevated offline ratios, reflecting heightened regulatory focus on exchange security following the largest crypto theft on record, which investigators have linked to a North Korean hacking group.

The new development follows hackers draining roughly $6.3 billion from Binance hot wallets across 10 blockchains in about 20 minutes on October 29th, 2026, marking the largest crypto theft on record. Investigators link the attack to North Korea’s TraderTraitor group, which compromised the exchange’s transaction-signing systems. About half the funds were frozen by stablecoin issuers. Binance has since pledged to cover all losses from its reserves and company funds.​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​

King Ghidorah Is Ready for Its Close-Up: How ‘Godzilla Minus Zero’ Cracked the Legendary Monster’s Next Evolution

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

“Godzilla Minus Zero” builds on the strong visual foundation — and/or the rubble of Tokyo’s Ginza district — that director Takashi Yamazaki pounded into his previous film, “Godzilla Minus One.” Like that first film, “Minus Zero” is in conversation with the big ideas and cultural/emotional resonances behind Toho’s giant monsters, and it’s attuned to the beloved visual details of the franchise, going all the way back to the original “Godzilla” and the Shōwa era.

But historically, for all kinds of budgetary and technological reasons, King Ghidorah is usually not ready for his close-up.

The amount of time that “Godzilla Minus Zero” spends with its three-headed mutant space dragon on the ground is a notable departure from how Ghidorah is most often depicted: in flight, or towering far above Godzilla. Even in closer shots in a CGI-rich film like “Godzilla: King of the Monsters,” when all three heads get ready to chomp up a puny human or 10, the emphasis is on Ghidorah’s scale. Three heads are more terrifying than one. Related Stories Rejected Twice by Sundance, ‘Mouse’ Is One of the Year’s Best Movies. Why Aren’t More People Talking About It? From Los Javis to Lord and Miller, Director Duos Are a High-Risk, High-Reward Prospect in the Oscar Race

But “Godzilla Minus Zero” spends a lot of time with just one head, or focused on just one head, and its Ghidorah is as scary on the ground as it is in the air, and this is in part due to the particular design experience that director Yamazaki and his team brought to the world’s deadliest gold dragon. Yamazaki was tapped to design “Godzilla the Ride” at Seibuen Amusement Park (its full name is Godzilla the Ride: Giant Monsters Ultimate Battle) in 2021 and therefore had to figure out a version of Ghidorah that would work from the other side of a safety rail.

Yamazaki and animation studio Shirogumi worked on a monster concept that would feel, according to “Minus One” and “Minus Zero” producer Kazuaki Kishida, “as though the audience was under attack by Ghidorah itself. So it had to be very scary and very close-up,” Kishida told IndieWire. “[In the ride,] we see Ghidorah basically crawling on the ground with its neck, because it has a very long neck, and then really going after the audience. To bring King Ghidorah very close to humans and bring the eyeline closer was a huge core concept for this. So there was a lot of research that had gone into the ride that we were able to apply to ‘Godzilla Minus Zero.'”

The film uses Ghidorah’s eyeline to great effect. One of the most unnerving moments in its initial attack on Matsumoto City, as scientists Kanji Murakami (Min Tanaka) and Akira Hagiwara (Ko Maehara) scramble to both document the new monster on the block and survive the utter destruction of whole city blocks, is a moment of getting really, uncomfortably close to Ghidorah’s eye.

That forced proximity to Ghidorah makes all the spectacular space-dragon stuff it can do even more effective, though these effects are also as grounded in the natural and the tangible as much as possible. For Ghidorah’s destructive beam, for instance, visual effects artist Tatsuji Nojima did more research than even he thought he could do on how lightning visualizes itself.

“Everyone sort of recognized the string of light, that line of light. But if you look at lightning in slow motion, it leaves a line of something that lingers — this is apparently just water vaporizing instantly and turning into plasma,” Nojima told IndieWire. “I don’t fully know the science behind it, but there’s a visual effect that lightning leaves behind. So for Ghidorah’s destruction beam, I wanted to emphasize that element of it. I scatter particles around, kind of blur the imagery, and then let it linger longer than it normally would.”

Of course, whenever the destruction beam hits something, Nojima and the VFX team got to build the explosion. But it’s that visual sense of Ghidorah’s power lingering, and of getting too close to where Ghidorah can see you, that makes this version of the monster feel so dangerous.

“Godzilla Minus Zero” is now playing in theaters.

If you’re looking for a well-equipped OLED at a mid-range price, the discounted 55-inch Samsung S90H is worth considering, especially if you’re a PC gamer or watch in a room prone to glare. It offers a stronger feature set than the S85H without requiring the jump to the more expensive S95H. Just compare the S90F’s current price before buying, particularly if movie nights in a dark room are your priority.

Clouds Were Once Photography’s Greatest Challenge

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

The Met Museum’s “Chasing Clouds” is a pleasing romp through the technical problem of capturing clouds at photography’s beginning.

The original 1996 Windows XP default background is arguably the most viewed photo of all time; its cumulus puffs drifting across a way-too-blue sky are seared into my and countless others’ Y2K childhoods. Such images of fluffy clouds are so ubiquitous that it’s hard to believe they once presented one of photography’s greatest technological challenges. The Met exhibition Chasing Clouds is a pleasing romp through the history of this problem through a union of art and science, spanning the early 1800s to 1929 — roughly photography’s first century. While the medium showed promise as “the pencil of nature,” its first processes were overly sensitive to blue light, registering the sky as a blank white space, impossibly overexposed.

While this lent some images a minimalist mystique, like John Beasley Greene’s “The Nile in Front of the Theban Hills” (1853–54), other photographers resorted to painting clouds into their landscapes by hand. An initial breakthrough came when French photographer Gustave Le Gray figured out how to stitch together two pictures — one calibrated to sky, and the other to land and sea. (Photographic fakery is as old as the medium itself.)

Though the exhibition begins with painted cloud studies and ends with photographic modernism, visual discoveries unfold in a dialogue between painting and technology rather than as a scientific march from one to the other. This makes for a much more interesting story; obsolescence is often a capitalist public relations trick.

Tokenization platform Securitize gains 10% postmarket on Q3 results, move into asset-backed finance

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Tokenization platform Securitize climbed about 10% postmarket after reporting third quarter growth alongside increased operating expenses, and announcing an expansion into asset-backed finance through a strategic acquisition.

Miami-based Securitize, which made its public market debut in July, operates in the relatively new corner of the crypto equity universe involved in institutional tokenization infrastructure. Tokenized assets on the platform grew 9% in the third quarter, to $4.8 billion, from the same period a year ago. Aggregate transaction volume rose 20% from a year ago, to $4.3 billion. Revenue of $11.5 million was up 28% from last year.

The growth came as bullish sentiment around tokenization gained traction throughout the third quarter, ahead of the Security and Exchange Commission formalizing the long awaited Innovation Exemption on Sept. 17, which created a temporary pathway for trading of certain tokenized stocks and strengthened the regulatory outlook.

Profitability is still meaningfully below year-ago levels, due in part to costs associated with going public over the summer, as well as the scaling up of the tokenization business at an active time for the entire sector, Securitize CEO Carlos Domingo told CNBC in an exclusive interview.

Securitize reinforced "certain areas of the company like finance, compliance, legal [and] cybersecurity," which increased corporate costs "compared to a year ago when we were privately held," Domingo said. "We're in in a very good position. The adjusted EBITDA … is improving significantly from previous quarters."

Securitize reported an adjusted EBITDA loss of $2 million, compared with positive adjusted EBITDA of $3.6 million a year ago, better than a $5.5 million loss in the second quarter. The company posted a 183% increase in operating costs and expenses.

Also on Monday, the Securitize board authorized a $50 million share buyback and announced the acquisition of River One, an institutional asset-backed financing platform developing technology to connect stablecoin capital with traditional lending markets. Terms were not disclosed.

Securitize sees an opportunity to modernize the $9 trillion market for asset backed finance by using stablecoins to finance real world assets, generate yield and tokenize investment positions and credit products, Domingo said.The acquisition also marks an expansion beyond Securitize's core niche markets at a time when tokenization's competitive landscape is growing, with traditional asset managers like BlackRock and Franklin Templeton, and established financial market infrastructure providers like DTCC and Broadridge, entering the industry.

"We have been very active in tokenized funds, treasuries [and] yield … And we've been obviously recently very active in tokenized stocks, so this adds another piece of the puzzle in the emerging tokenization space for us to capture revenue growth."

How Often Should You Be Cleaning Your Leather Furniture? It's More Often Than You Think

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Every item on this page was hand-picked by a House Beautiful editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Pictured above: A leather seat in a space designed by Jill Tran of Tran + Thomas Design Studio.

No one invests in a piece of leather furniture with plans to replace it again in just a few years. After all, a full-grain leather sofa can go decades without cracking, and minor scuffs simply add to the patina.

But the key lies in how well you care for your leather furniture. Leather may be lower maintenance than fabric upholstery, but it still needs occasional TLC to last.

We reached out to textile experts for tips on how to extend the life of leather furniture. Here's how often you should clean and condition these pieces, and how to tell the difference between normal patina and damage that needs attention.

Lisa Beach is the director of operations at BenchMade Modern, a furniture company that specializes in leather. According to her, cleaning your leather furniture regularly protects it from damage.

She recommends vacuuming your leather furniture and wiping it down with a dry cloth any time you notice dust, pet hair and dander, or crumbs starting to accumulate. This can range from every one to two weeks, depending on how often you use the furniture. The goal is to prevent these particles from settling into the furniture and possibly scratching the surface.

In general, she warns against using a damp cloth during these quick and frequent cleanings. "For semi-aniline or protected leathers, you may wipe down your furniture every few months with a damp cloth, but be sure to follow with a dry cloth to remove any excess moisture," she says. "Natural full-grain leathers are more absorbent, so we don't recommend using damp cloths or moisture unless you need to clean a spill or stain."

Leather furniture also benefits from a deeper clean with a specialized pH-balanced product every few months.

Shawn McGowen is the CEO of Leather Honey, a family-owned company that has been producing leather care products since 1968. He suggests blocking off time for a deep clean every three to six months, and following it up with a leather conditioner.

Leather naturally ages and develops a patina over time. McGowen says patinaed leather shows off a slightly darker color, softer sheen and smoother feel. You'll first notice this starting to develop in the places you sit and rest your arms most often.

"This is where your natural body oils slowly burnish the leather’s surface, giving that lived-in look that people seek after," he says. "On the flip side, damage often occurs due to neglect and looks more like cracking, flaking, stiffness, or wrinkling that doesn’t go away with cleaning. Patina comes from properly using your leather while damage comes from neglect."

Note that leather furniture can fade instead of darken with age if it sits in a particularly sunny room. However, this doesn't mean you need to keep the curtains drawn or move your leather furniture to a darker corner. McGowen says that at-home recoloring balms or leather dyes are very easy to apply to small areas.

Even if you're a very determined DIYer, it's important to know when it's best to reach out to a leather care professional to restore your furniture. Otherwise, you may end up doing more harm than good.

"When leather becomes too far gone and starts to show signs of neglect or wear such as tears, holes, large areas of discoloration, or stains, that is when the DIY becomes more difficult and potentially a waste of time," McGowen says. "These should often be handled by professional leather care specialists because they have years of experience in color matching and upholstery, and have a wide range of tools needed for larger restoration projects."

Man who lost $308,000 on sports bets sues Kalshi for offering illegal gambling

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Lawsuit demands Kalshi return losses because online betting is illegal in Washington.

A man who lost over $308,000 making sports wagers on Kalshi is suing the prediction market for violating the state of Washington’s ban on online gambling. He’s trying to turn those losses into an overall victory with a lawsuit seeking a payment of over $580,000.

Bellevue, Washington, resident Scott Bright’s “wagering occurred at a time when it was unlawful in Washington and when the Washington Attorney General had already sued KalshiEX LLC in this Court alleging precisely that,” said the gambler’s lawsuit filed on September 24 in King County Superior Court. Kalshi has since filed a motion to dismiss the lawsuit.

While state attorneys general throughout the US are trying to shut down Kalshi for allegedly violating state gambling laws, Bright’s case tests whether unsuccessful gamblers can obtain restitution from the company through individual lawsuits.

“Washington has prohibited Internet gambling since 2006. Washington has also provided, since long before that, a statutory cause of action permitting any person who loses money at illegal gambling to recover it from the proprietor for whose benefit the game was conducted,” Bright’s lawsuit said.

Even if Bright’s lawsuit fails, gamblers who lost on Kalshi could get money back through the lawsuits filed by state governments. For example, Washington’s lawsuit against the company said the attorney general “is entitled to recover all money Washingtonians have lost through Defendant Kalshi’s illegal gambling operation” under the state’s Recovery of Money Lost at Gambling Act. Washington already won a court order restricting the kinds of wagers Kalshi can offer in the state, but it took effect after Bright’s losses.

Kalshi wants Bright’s lawsuit to be moved to US District Court for the Western District of Washington, saying it must be tried in a federal court instead of a state one. In an October 30 motion to dismiss Bright’s case, Kalshi argued that he did not lose any money on “gambling.” Kalshi said he lost money on “event contracts,” which Kalshi says can only be regulated by the federal government.

“The contracts which Plaintiff mischaracterizes as gambling are event contracts offered on the Platform subject to the CEA [Commodity Exchange Act], in conformance with the rules and regulations of the CFTC [Commodity Futures Trading Commission], the federal agency which has statutory authority to oversee the CEA,” Kalshi said.

We contacted Bright’s attorney, Michael Daudt, about Kalshi’s court filings and will update this article if we get a response.

“Plaintiff alleges that his activity on the platform was gambling and that he ‘lost,’” Kalshi wrote. “He does not allege that anyone misled him about how the platform worked, what it charged, or the risk that any position might lose. Yet, he now sues KalshiEX and its parent, Kalshi Inc., to recover nearly double his own alleged net realized loss under Washington’s gambling loss-recovery statute, and a theory of unjust enrichment.”

Between June 17 and August 14, 2026, Bright transferred $315,353.94 to Kalshi in 253 separate debits from his checking account, his lawsuit said. Kalshi allegedly collected $5,589.05 in funding fees on those transfers.

Bright made numerous wagers on sporting events including professional baseball games and tennis matches. He apparently put any winnings into new bets, and alleges that “Kalshi’s platform cycled Plaintiff’s deposits through $14,396,457.30 in transactions and assessed and retained $84,062.71 in trading fees on them, equal to 26.7 percent of everything Plaintiff deposited.”

After all his bets, Bright suffered a net loss of $308,781.62, and his account was left with a balance of $983.27. Kalshi said that Bright’s “alleged losses went to counterparties rather than” to Kalshi itself and that Bright’s “positions were matched with counterparties and resolved according to the outcomes of the underlying events.”

“The Complaint does not allege that KalshiEX or Kalshi Inc. was the counterparty to any of Plaintiff’s positions,” Kalshi said.

Kalshi calls itself “a neutral exchange platform” with no financial stake in the outcome of any event. It makes revenue by collecting fees on transactions. But while Kalshi describes itself as a neutral operator and collector of transaction fees, its business model also includes a trading affiliate that places wagers on the platform.

Kalshi’s rulebook says its trading affiliate “participates on the Platform and provides liquidity to the Platform by placing orders on either (or both) sides of a market at competitive price levels.” Kalshi says its affiliate only has access to the same public information that other traders on the platform have.

Bright’s lawsuit alleges that Kalshi’s affiliate “took the opposite side of a substantial portion of Plaintiff’s positions,” meaning that “Kalshi and the enterprise under common ownership with it were not a neutral venue but the dealer or player winning within the meaning of [state law], in addition to being the proprietor for whose benefit the wagering was conducted.”

Bright doesn’t know for sure whether Kalshi’s affiliate was on the other side of any of his bets. “The identity of the participant on the opposite side of each of Plaintiff’s positions, and the profit each such participant realized, is information exclusively within Kalshi’s possession,” Bright’s lawsuit said.

The US Court of Appeals for the Ninth Circuit ruled in August that Nevada can regulate Kalshi under gambling laws. Although that case involved Nevada, the Ninth Circuit’s jurisdiction includes the state of Washington. Bright’s lawsuit quoted the ruling’s finding that “placing sports bets, even when called by another name, is still gambling.”

Although Bright’s lawsuit said his net loss was $308,781.62, he asked for $580,544.98 based on an “event-by-event” calculation derived from a 1950 ruling issued by the Washington Supreme Court. “Computed on the event-by-event basis required by Heitfeld, under which winnings in separate and distinct events may not be offset against losses, the sums Plaintiff lost total $580,544.98,” the lawsuit said.

Bright’s lawsuit said that if the court declines to order $580,544.98 in restitution, it should instead award “all funds Plaintiff transferred to Kalshi and the $89,651.76 in funding and trading fees Kalshi collected.” That would amount to over $405,000.

In addition to its motion to dismiss the case, Kalshi filed a motion to stay the proceedings at least until the US Supreme Court decides whether to take up a separate case on whether state gambling laws apply to prediction markets. “The orderly course of justice favors a stay because Supreme Court review will settle or simplify every claim and ensure uniform treatment of like suits,” Kalshi told the US district court.

Kalshi has suffered some notable losses in US appeals courts, which found that states can regulate the platform’s event contracts under gambling laws. But federal courts have not unanimously sided with states, and New Jersey asked the Supreme Court to settle the question of whether states can regulate Kalshi under gambling laws.

Kalshi’s motion for a stay in the Bright case noted that Washington government officials are “pursuing overlapping claims” against Kalshi. “The State of Washington is pursuing the same theory, and seeks the same recovery for Mr. Bright, in a case pending before the Superior Court of Washington for King County,” Kalshi wrote.

Kalshi tried to get the state’s case moved to a federal court, but it was sent back to King County Superior Court in May. Washington won a preliminary injunction against Kalshi in August, when a King County judge ordered Kalshi to implement geofencing and stop allowing many types of wagers in the state.

The county court’s order required Kalshi to stop offering or accepting event contracts “related to sports, elections, politics, entertainment, culture, tech and science, or mentions” in the state. The injunction did not affect wagers in the commodities, climate, economics, and finance categories.

Bright transferred his funds to Kalshi before the geofencing order took effect. His lawsuit claims that on September 15, he tried to access the app but received a notice that “using this app in your current location is prohibited.”

This article was updated to correct the date the lawsuit was filed.

After all, you don’t need to know everything, only what’s important.

Lucid misses Wall Street’s earnings expectations, expands loan from Saudi Arabia fund

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Lucid Group missed Wall Street's third-quarter expectations as the embattled electric vehicle maker continues an "operational reset" aimed at cutting costs and narrowing losses.

The company on Monday also announced a $1.75 billion increase in a delayed draw term loan from Saudi Arabia's Public Investment Fund, the company's largest shareholder.

Lucid stock fell roughly 5% in aftermarket trading Monday.

Here's how Lucid performed in the third quarter compared with average estimates compiled by LSEG:

Despite the top- and bottom-line misses, the company's adjusted earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation and amortization of a loss of $726 million beat Wall Street's expectations of a loss of $611 million for the third quarter. Its net loss for the quarter was $830.9 million, down from a loss of $978.4 million a year earlier.

Lucid's 2025 third quarter included revenue of $336.6 million, a loss of $3.31 per share and an adjusted EBITDA loss of $717.7 million.

The quarterly results are the first since Lucid CEO Silvio Napoli, who started leading the company in June, announced a restructuring plan that included implementing several cost-cutting efforts and delaying the company's upcoming midsize vehicle from the end of this year until the second half of next.

"Our transformation is beginning to show progress. We are acting with urgency, strengthening the fundamentals and driving greater discipline, while continuing to invest in the priorities to deliver profitable long-term growth," Napoli said in a release.

The company's turnaround plan includes $1.4 billion in cash flow improvements this year as well as focusing attention on a robotaxi program with Uber and Nuro, a factory that's under construction in Saudi Arabia and the midsize vehicle.

Lucid said the capital from PIF is expected to give the company "additional financial flexibility as Lucid delivers on its turnaround program." It is expected to assist in funding "operations and key investments" into the fourth quarter of 2027, including the start of production for the midsize vehicle.

The additional funds boost Lucid's total liquidity to $3.75 billion, up from $2 billion to end the third quarter.

"We are grateful for the continued financial support from our anchor shareholder, which further energizes us to remain focused on execution and on our commitments as we build a stronger, sustainable Lucid," Napoli said.

Lucid said Monday that the company expects to communicate its midterm plan and targets early next year. The EV maker in May suspended its guidance and many targets, citing new leadership and time needed to develop a turnaround plan.

Is Your Sodium Intake Impacting Your Hiking Performance? A Dietitian Explains Why It Matters.

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Take a look at the nutrition label on a freeze-dried backpacking meal, and you might be surprised by how much sodium it contains. Some meals pack more than 1,500 mg into a single pouch, roughly two-thirds of the recommended daily limit of 2,300 mg for most adults. That’s a lot for one dinner, but when you’re spending hours sweating your way up steep climbs, you’re also losing sodium along with water.

Sodium helps regulate fluid balance and supports normal nerve and muscle function. Yet there’s a contradiction in how you might think about it on the trail: you question the sodium in your freeze-dried dinner while adding electrolyte powder to your water bottle and snacking on pretzels, jerky, and cheese.

So, are you consuming too much sodium or not enough? The answer depends on what’s already in your food, how much you’re sweating, and how you’re replacing fluids.

You can pack nearly identical amounts of food and end up with dramatically different sodium intakes. I compared two realistic days of backpacking food, each containing commercial meals, protein bars, snacks, and other familiar trail staples. Both provide roughly 3,150 calories and weigh about 23 ounces dry, yet one contains 5,465 mg of sodium while the other contains just 1,975 mg.

That’s nearly 3,500 mg of difference before you add a single electrolyte packet. Some of the variation comes down to individual products: Mountain House Beef Lasagna contains 1,180 mg of sodium, compared with 340 mg in Good To-Go Pad Thai. Even switching pretzel flavors or protein bar brands can make a noticeable difference. Neither food bag is automatically better, but the comparison shows why it’s worth checking what you’re already carrying before deciding to supplement.

Health experts recommend limiting sodium for a reason. Consistently consuming more than your body needs can raise blood pressure, increasing your risk of heart disease and stroke over time. A particularly salty meal can also leave you thirsty or temporarily bloated as your body regulates its fluid balance. If you’ve been advised to limit sodium because of high blood pressure, kidney disease, or another medical condition, those recommendations still matter when you’re backpacking.

But eating a high-sodium diet day after day isn’t necessarily the same as eating salty meals during a strenuous backpacking trip. When you’re sweating heavily, you lose sodium you wouldn’t lose while sitting at a desk. That doesn’t mean every milligram of sodium in your food is necessary, or that you should add electrolytes without thinking about what you’re already eating. It simply means your activity and conditions matter when interpreting those numbers on the label.

Even if you’re hiking alongside someone on the same trail, you may be losing very different amounts of sodium. Sweat losses depend on two things: how much you sweat and how concentrated that sweat is. Research has found sweat sodium concentrations ranging from roughly 230 to 1,600 mg per liter. Temperature, exertion, clothing, heat acclimatization, and individual physiology all affect your losses, which means mileage alone tells you very little about how much sodium you’ve lost.

Here’s another wrinkle: losing 800 mg of sodium through sweat doesn’t automatically mean you need to consume 800 mg to replace it. Your body regulates sodium and water balance, and research suggests targeted sodium replacement matters more under certain combinations of prolonged exercise, substantial sweat losses, and fluid replacement. That’s why taking an electrolyte packet every hour isn’t necessarily appropriate. A packet doesn’t account for how much you’re sweating, how much you’re drinking, or how much sodium you’re getting from food.

Sodium doesn’t have to come from an electrolyte supplement to count. Salty snacks, meals, and electrolyte drinks all contribute to your intake, although when you consume that sodium can matter during long, sweaty days. Imagine spending ten hours hiking in warm weather, sweating heavily and drinking water but eating very little. At camp, you prepare a freeze-dried dinner containing 1,500 mg of sodium. That sodium helps replace losses and retain fluids during recovery, but it wasn’t available during the preceding hours of hiking.

Eating salty snacks throughout a prolonged, sweaty hike provides some sodium along the way instead of waiting until dinner. That doesn’t mean you need electrolyte powder in every water bottle. If you’re regularly eating pretzels, cheese, jerky, or other salty foods, you’re already consuming sodium during the day. On the other hand, if your trail menu contains relatively little sodium or you struggle to eat while hiking, an electrolyte drink may be a convenient alternative. What matters isn’t whether the sodium comes from a packet or a snack, but whether you’re adding something your food isn’t already providing.

It’s easy to assume that replacing the sodium you lose through sweat will protect you from exercise-associated hyponatremia (EAH), a potentially dangerous drop in blood sodium. But according to the Wilderness Medical Society, most cases occur primarily because you drink too much fluid relative to your losses, often combined with your body’s tendency to retain water during prolonged exercise. Sweat sodium losses can contribute under some circumstances, but taking electrolyte supplements won’t prevent hyponatremia if you’re overdrinking.

The guidelines recommend avoiding overdrinking, generally by drinking according to thirst or using fluid needs you’ve estimated from experience in similar conditions. They also recommend having salty foods or drinks available during long, hot activities. Headaches, fatigue, nausea, and muscle cramps aren’t reliable indicators that you need more sodium because they can have several causes. If you or a hiking partner develops worsening confusion, repeated vomiting, seizures, or collapses during or after prolonged exertion, seek urgent medical help rather than assuming more water or electrolytes will solve the problem.

There’s no simple answer. Your sweat losses vary with the conditions and your individual physiology. Even knowing exactly how much sodium you lose wouldn’t tell you how much to replace. For context, 1,500 mg per day is considered an adequate intake for most adults under ordinary conditions, while 2,300 mg is the general recommended daily limit. Neither is a backpacking-specific target. A food bag containing 5,465 mg isn’t automatically excessive for a strenuous hiking day, just as one containing 1,975 mg isn’t necessarily inadequate. And since research hasn’t consistently shown that extra sodium improves endurance performance, there’s little reason to chase a precise replacement target.

The bottom line? Hike your own hike. If you’re curious about your sodium intake, track what you eat and drink, the conditions you’re hiking in, and how you feel during and afterward. You might notice patterns that help you plan future trips, without getting caught up in replacing every milligram of sodium you sweat out.

A Kitchen in Bloom: Inside Miele’s Lush Botanical Oasis Where Art Meets Design

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Step inside Art Basel as visionaries Tiffany Howell and Asher Young merge cutting-edge technology with untamed botanical romance, staging an immersive sensory landscape you don’t just see, but feel.

Miele crafts appliances for enduring performance, but they also curate experiences that engage all the senses. Imagine the intuitive precision of a sleek induction cooktop that subtly adjusts temperature on its own; the soft, inviting glow of a French door refrigerator that reveals a curated bounty within ample space; the alluring aroma of a built-in coffee system greeting you each morning; and the calming, almost meditative hum of a steam at work. Each of these choreographed moments elevate Miele appliances beyond a functional tool into something you fundamentally feel. By blending groundbreaking innovation and sustainable features with timeless, minimalist design, Miele frees you to embrace the pure joy of cooking and to savor the intimate, everyday moments that make a house a true home.

For this year’s Art Basel, the ultimate intersection of culture and creativity, Miele brings together two visionaries to redefine the culinary space. Tiffany Howell of Night Palm Design is passionate about creating cinematic homes that tell a deeply personal story. Asher Young is a multidisciplinary artist known for merging art with technology to create immersive spectacle. Together, they have imagined a sensory kitchen built entirely within a lush botanical garden setting. Here, the wildness of the natural world frames the precise German engineering. It is a space where the smooth, handleless silhouettes and cool touch of Miele's Obsidian Black glass stand in striking relief against their organic surroundings, harnessing technology for a design that is both tactile and state-of-the-art. “We used the sleek, matte black Miele appliances as the driving force to bring this to life,” says Howell of the striking visual contrast between nature and cutting-edge machines. “They’re like the perfect little black dress—timeless, elegant, and grounding the entire room.” A deep respect for the environment is also reflected in Miele engineering, which uses innovative technology that preserves ingredients longer—ensuring that the natural world isn't just an aesthetic inspiration, but a core priority for the brand.

Howell’s signature nostalgic romance blends with Asher’s analytical thinking and knack for creating physical, interactive experiences to make the space come alive. Young's touch ensures the installation feels genuinely dynamic, translating the unseen power of Miele’s technology into a captivating physical experience. “How can we create a space that rebalances and also is a place that you can actually engage with? So you can smell the flowers while you're washing your hand. You can feel the cool breeze as you open the fridge,” explains Young. “All these elements is not just for the sake of a visual presentation, but actually to experience and be inside.” The result is an experiential reminder that kitchens should always reflect your personality and passions. “We both said let’s bring our shared language together,” he recalls. The finished design is the undeniable heartbeat of the garden, a space with one foot rooted elegantly in the past and the other stepping boldly into the future.

“A kitchen expresses how you live, what you love and desire, and it’s also where your most cherished memories are made,” says Howell. As the Miami sun sets, the botanical space seamlessly transitions into an elegant, moody enclave for evening entertaining, inviting you to linger just a little bit longer. And in a Miele kitchen, those memories continue into the next day, beginning the moment the fragrant aroma of freshly brewed coffee announces the limitless possibilities of a brand-new morning.

Experience the convergence of culinary art, design, and innovation firsthand. Explore the full Miele collection and begin designing a kitchen that awakens every sense.
